# Antigravity Skills Master Guide & Complete Catalog

This document provides a comprehensive operational manual for managing, activating, deactivating, and executing agent skills in Google Antigravity. It includes a complete catalog of all **2780 installed skills** (from `sickn33/agentic-awesome-skills`, `morluto/rea`, and `K-Dense-AI/scientific-agent-skills`).

## 1. Core Architecture: The Staging Pattern

Antigravity natively scans the directory `.agents/skills/` within your workspace.

```text
📁 /Users/md.rofazhasanrafiu/coding/
├── 📚 .skills-library/         <--- [WAREHOUSE] All 2,780+ skills stored locally (Zero context cost)
└── ⚡ .agents/
    └── 🎯 skills/             <--- [ACTIVE DESK] Only your actively linked skills loaded into Antigravity
```

- **`.skills-library/` (The Warehouse):** Contains all full skill definitions, references, scripts, and evaluation rubrics. Because Antigravity does **not** index this folder automatically, keeping thousands of skills here has **zero impact on token consumption or system speed**.
- **`.agents/skills/` (The Active Desk):** Contains only the skills you are actively using right now. Antigravity monitors this folder and injects these skills into its cognitive decision loop.

---

## 2. How to Turn Skills ON (Activation)

To activate any skill from the library, link or copy it into `.agents/skills/`.

### Method A: Interactive Prompt (Easiest)
Simply tell Antigravity in the chat:
> *"Activate the `docker-best-practices` and `systematic-debugging` skills."*

Antigravity will create the symlinks for you automatically.

### Method B: Symlink via Terminal (Recommended)
Symlinks make the skill active immediately without duplicating disk space:
```bash
ln -s "$(pwd)/.skills-library/<skill-name>" .agents/skills/
```

*Examples:*
```bash
# Activate Reverse Engineering & Binary Inspection
ln -s "$(pwd)/.skills-library/reverse-engineer-anything" .agents/skills/

# Activate Systematic Debugging
ln -s "$(pwd)/.skills-library/systematic-debugging" .agents/skills/

# Activate Docker Best Practices
ln -s "$(pwd)/.skills-library/docker-best-practices" .agents/skills/
```

### Method C: Physical Copy
If you prefer a standalone copy to modify:
```bash
cp -r .skills-library/<skill-name> .agents/skills/
```

---

## 3. How to Turn Skills OFF (Deactivation)

When your task is complete, deactivate the skill to keep your prompt context clean and focused.

### Method A: Interactive Prompt
Simply tell Antigravity:
> *"Deactivate `reverse-engineer-anything`."*

### Method B: Terminal Command
```bash
# Deactivate a single skill:
rm .agents/skills/<skill-name>

# Deactivate all active skills (clean slate):
rm -rf .agents/skills/*
```

> **Note:** Deactivating a skill from `.agents/skills/` **never deletes it** from your `.skills-library/`. The skill remains safe in the catalog for future use.

---

## 4. How Skills Execute & What Outputs They Produce

### The 4-Phase Execution Lifecycle

```text
[User Prompt] 
      │
      ▼
1. Match & Identify ──► Antigravity reads active SKILL.md frontmatter (`name` + `description`)
      │
      ▼
2. Ingest Playbook  ──► Antigravity reads `SKILL.md` instructions & scripts via `view_file`
      │
      ▼
3. Step Execution   ──► Performs exact operational steps, diagnostic probes, and tools
      │
      ▼
4. Output & Verify  ──► Generates verified artifacts, reports, diffs, or code implementations
```

---

### Output Archetypes

| Archetype | How It Performs | Typical Output Produced | Example Skills |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **🛡️ Auditing & Security** | Runs automated linting, probes vulnerabilities, checks WCAG or OWASP benchmarks. | Severity matrix table, vulnerability breakdown, remediation patch diffs. | `reverse-engineer-anything`, `accessibility-compliance-accessibility-audit`, `rea-ci-tests-audit` |
| **🏗️ Architecture & Generation** | Follows strict patterns to structure schemas, APIs, components, or database models. | Production-ready boilerplate, TypeScript types, validation schemas, unit tests. | `zod-validation-expert`, `rea-tool-design`, `nextjs-app-router` |
| **🔬 Diagnostics & Reverse Eng** | Inspects binaries, Electron ASARs, network pcap/saz captures, or memory crashes. | Evidence ledger, decompiled pseudocode, call graphs, protocol frame decoders. | `reverse-engineer-anything`, `wireshark-analysis`, `web2-recon` |
| **🧪 Testing & Experimentation** | Sets up test fixtures, statistical sample sizes, mocks, or assertions. | Full test suite (`vitest`/`jest`), A/B test runbooks, statistical power tables. | `ab-testing`, `webapp-testing`, `rea-ci-tests-audit` |
| **⚡ Performance & Optimization** | Identifies bottlenecks, memory leaks, layout shifts, or unoptimized queries. | Flame graphs, Core Web Vitals audit, optimized SQL queries, refactored code. | `web-performance-optimization`, `rea-analysis-optimization` |
| **🔬 Scientific & Laboratory** | Computes molecular structures, runs genomic lookups, aligns sequences, models ODE/flux systems. | PDB/CIF 3D models, flux distributions, VEP reports, publication-grade figures. | `alphagenome`, `cobrapy`, `astropy`, `biopython` |

## 5. Quick Helper Script (`skill-mgr`)

You can add this quick helper function to your `~/.zshrc` (or run it in your terminal) to manage skills instantly:

```bash
# Add to ~/.zshrc or execute in terminal:
skill() {
  case "$1" in
    on)
      ln -s "$(pwd)/.skills-library/$2" .agents/skills/ 2>/dev/null && echo "✅ Activated: $2" || echo "❌ Skill not found: $2"
      ;;
    off)
      rm -f ".agents/skills/$2" 2>/dev/null && echo "🛑 Deactivated: $2" || echo "❌ Skill was not active: $2"
      ;;
    list)
      echo "⚡ Currently Active Skills in .agents/skills:"
      ls -1 .agents/skills
      ;;
    search)
      echo "🔍 Search results in .skills-library:"
      ls -1 .skills-library | grep -i "$2"
      ;;
    off-all)
      rm -rf .agents/skills/*
      echo "🧹 All skills deactivated."
      ;;
    *)
      echo "Usage: skill [on <name> | off <name> | list | search <query> | off-all]"
      ;;
  esac
}
```
